\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda)=0
. Especially,
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda)
coincides with the definition (
1.22
). Then Corollary
4.3
also defines a limit

(5.2)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho):=2\chi(\rho){\overbracket{D}}(\rho)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}).

In this section, we are ready to prove the convergence rate.

Proposition 5.1
.

There exists an exponent
\gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0
and a positive constant
{C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty}
such that for every
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\right|+\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\right|\right)\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{1}}.

Although Proposition
5.1
is stated for
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})
and
\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})
, we will still rely on
{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,\Lambda)
and
{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda)
in the intermediate steps as they are already normalized; see (2) of Proposition
4.1
. Note that
\chi(\rho)
in
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})
helps to prove the uniform convergence in
\rho\in[0,1]
. Throughout this section, we define the following shorthand expression

(5.4)

\displaystyle D_{n}:={\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}).

By the subadditive quantity (
4.6
) and
(
4.7
)
, we know that
{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
and
{\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
are decreasing. Therefore, it suffices to show the convergence rate for
|{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})-{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})|
, which is reduced to the decay rate of
\sup_{|p|=1}J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,D_{m}p)
after applying (
4.19
) with
\widetilde{D}=D_{m}
.

In this section, we will heavily use the master quantity
J(\rho,\Lambda,p,q)
defined in (
4.16
) and its optimizer
v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p,q)
defined in (1) of Proposition
4.7
. The notation
\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}=3^{n}{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\cap{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}
is usually involved to make comparison between
J
in different scales. We will also use the following gap of the master quantities very often in the proof

(5.5)

\tau_{n}:=\sup_{p,q\in B_{1}}(J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,q)-J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n+1},p,q)),

where
B_{1}:=\{p\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}}:|p|=1\}
.

Our first lemma makes use of the elliptic regularity, especially the modified Caccioppoli inequality (
2.10
), in our master quantity.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a finite positive constant
C(d,\lambda)
such that, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
satisfying
3^{m}>R_{0}
for the constant in Proposition
2.6
, we have the following estimate

(5.6)

\displaystyle J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-m}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}v(\cdot,\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We write
v_{m}=v(\cdot,\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)
as a shortened version in the proof. Recall the operator
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}
defined in (
2.9
), and denote by

(5.7)

\displaystyle L:=3^{m}+2\mathbf{r},

as the length of localization in this proof with
3^{m}>2\mathbf{r}
. Then, by (
4.22
), we decompose our quantity
J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)
as

(5.8)

\begin{split}&J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant(\mathbf{I})^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\mathbf{II})^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
\mathbf{I}&:=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho},\\
\mathbf{II}&:=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split}

We justify at first
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
by testing an arbitrary function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{-})

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{{\mathsf{A}}_{L}\left(c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)\right)}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0.

Here in the first line, since
c_{b}
and
f
are both
\mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{L}}
-measurable, they commute with
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}
operator. In the second line, we use the fact that
v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
from (1) of Proposition
4.7
.

We now turn to the two terms in (
5.8
). As we have proved
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
, we apply (
4.23
) to
\mathbf{I}
and get

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}=J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}).

We investigate the expression of
J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})
by (
4.17
). By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho},

and by the definition of conditional expectation, we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}(c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{p})(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})-(\pi_{b}\ell_{D_{m}p})(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}))}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}(c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{p})(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})-(\pi_{b}\ell_{D_{m}p})(\pi_{b}v_{m+1}))}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

These imply that
J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})\geqslant J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1})
. Putting it back to (
5.9
) and using (
4.23
) again, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle\leqslant J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1})

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

We claim that the right hand side of the inequality can be controlled by
C\tau_{m}
defined in (
5.5
) with some constant only depending on the dimension
d
. It suffices to apply (
4.23
) to
z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}
with
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m}
. We denote by
v_{m,z}=v(\cdot,\rho,z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)
to simplify the notation in the following calculation

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m,z}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m}}\left(J(\rho,z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1})\right)

\displaystyle=3^{d}(J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p)).

This concludes that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant 3^{d}\lambda\tau_{m}.

Concerning
\mathbf{II}
, as we have verified the condition in Proposition
2.6
that
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
and
3^{m}>R_{0}
is assumed, we can apply the modified Caccioppoli inequality (
2.10
) to
{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}
to get

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{C3^{-2m}}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}v_{m+1}^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}+\theta J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p).

Here the factor
\theta\in(0,1)
and the constant
C
all come from Proposition
2.6
, and only depends on
d,\lambda
.

Combining the two estimates (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we have

J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-m}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}v({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Noting the fact
\theta\in(0,1)
, we rearrange the expression above and conclude (
5.6
).
∎

5.1.
Flatness estimate

This part gives a flatness estimate and this is the main challenge compared to its counterpart in
[
35
, Lemma 5.1]
. The whole Section
3
is devoted to overcome the technical difficulty here.

Proposition 5.3

(
L^{2}
-flatness estimate)
.

There exists an exponent
\beta(d)\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
and a constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that for every
p,q\in B_{1}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.12)

\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}\left\langle{\left(v(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,q)-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
\leqslant C\alpha(\rho)3^{2m}\left(\rho^{-2}\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right)+\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}\right).

Proof.

In the proof, we write
v_{m+1}
as a shorthand of
v(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,q)
. We apply the weighted multiscale Poincaré inequality in Proposition
3.9
to obtain

(5.13)

\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant C\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho)\sum_{n=1}^{m}3^{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the term
I_{z,n}
is the shorthand of

I_{z,n}:=\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p})}\right\rangle_{z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+},\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right|^{2},

and
\mathbf{N}_{z,n}
is the same as defined in Proposition
3.9
. Recall (
1.50
) for
\nabla_{x}
. We need to treat the terms
I_{z,n}
and study at first the case of large scales.

Case 1.1: large scale
n>\frac{m}{2(d+1)}
; high density
\mathbf{N}_{z,n}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|
.
For this case, the weight is of typical size and does not degenerate, so we have

(5.14)

\begin{split}&\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\mathbf{N}_{z,n}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|\right\}}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
&\leqslant\left\langle{\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\mathbf{N}_{z,n}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|\right\}}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
&\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\right)^{2}\left\langle{I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split}

Here we use the fact
I_{z,n}\geqslant 0
, so we can drop the indicator in the third line. Then we use the comparison of Dirichlet energy with
v_{n,z}=v(\rho,z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,q)

(5.15)

\begin{split}\left\langle{I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}&\leqslant\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})}\right\rangle_{z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+},\mathbf{N}_{z}}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
&\quad+\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})}\right\rangle_{z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+},\mathbf{N}_{z}}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
&\quad+3|D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q|^{2}.\end{split}

We will estimate the first term by Lemma
5.4
below, which studies the decay of variance

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})}\right\rangle_{z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+},\mathbf{N}_{z}}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant C3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}.

Here from the first line to the second line, we use Jensen’s inequality. Notice that the exponent
\beta
only depends on
d
and the constant
C
depends on
\lambda,\mathbf{r},d
as stated in Lemma
5.4
.

The second term in (
5.15
) can be controlled by the gap of the subadditive quantity using (
4.23
) as the proof of Lemma
5.6

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})}\right\rangle_{z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+},\mathbf{N}_{z}}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,q)-J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1},p,q)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=n}^{m}\tau_{k}.

The third term in (
5.15
) is also naturally bounded

\displaystyle|D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q|^{2}\leqslant C(\lambda)\sum_{k=n}^{m-1}\tau_{k}.

We combine these three terms and take in account of the factor
\rho^{-2}
from (
5.14
) to obtain,

(5.16)

\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\mathbf{N}_{z,n}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|\right\}}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
\leqslant C\rho^{-2}\left(3^{-\beta n}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}+\sum_{k=n}^{m}\tau_{k}\right).

Case 1.2: large scale
n>\frac{m}{2(d+1)}
; low density
1\leqslant\mathbf{N}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|
.
For this case, we will get a very large factor from the weight using the trivial bound

(5.17)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}\leqslant 3^{2dn}.

However, this case is quite rare by Hoeffding inequality (see
[
18
, Theorem 2.8]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{N}_{z,n}<\frac{\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right]\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right).

We also make use of the
L^{\infty}
estimate from Lemma
4.4
, and obtain

(5.18)

\begin{split}\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{1\leqslant\mathbf{N}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|\right\}}}\right\rangle_{\rho}&\leqslant\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{2dn}\left\|{v_{m+1}}\right\|^{2}_{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{N}_{z,n}<\frac{\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right]\\
&\leqslant C(\lambda,d)\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{2dn}3^{2(d+3)m}\exp\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right)\\
&\leqslant C(\lambda,d)\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{2dn}3^{2(d+3)m}\exp\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}\right).\end{split}

In the last line, we use the condition
n>\frac{m}{2(d+1)}
to bound the rare probability.

Now we turn to the case of small scales.

Case 2: small scale
0\leqslant n\leqslant\frac{m}{2(d+1)}
.
For this case, we cannot expect too much spatial average cancellation, while the concentration effect is not strong enough to beat the
L^{\infty}
norm like in (
5.18
). On the other hand, the factors from the Poincaré inequality and the weight is still not so large. Therefore, we use Jensen’s inequality and the trivial bound (
5.17
), and pay directly the price of the volume
|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant 3^{2dn}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}|\nabla_{x}v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p}|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

The contribution from all these scales are

(5.19)

\begin{split}&\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho)\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2(d+1)}\rfloor}3^{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\left\langle{\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}|}{2\mathbf{N}_{z,n}}\right)^{2}I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leqslant\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho)\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2(d+1)}\rfloor}3^{(d+1)n}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}\sum_{x\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}|\nabla_{x}v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p}|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leqslant C(\lambda)\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho)3^{\frac{m}{2}}.\end{split}

Here from the second line to the third line, we just use the Dirichlet energy estimate of
\nabla_{x}v_{m+1}
in (2) of Proposition
4.1
.

Plugging (
5.16
), (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) into (
5.13
), we have

\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant C(\lambda)\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho)\left(3^{\frac{m}{2}}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{m}{2(d+1)}\rfloor}^{m}3^{n}\left(\rho^{-2}\left(3^{-\beta n}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}+\sum_{k=n}^{m}\tau_{k}\right)\right.\right.\\
\left.\left.+\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{2dn}3^{2(d+3)m}\exp\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Square this equation with Cauchy–Schwartz inequality, and shrink
\beta
, then we prove (
5.12
).

∎

5.2.
Variance decay of the averaged gradient

In this part, we prove the variance decay of gradient. Since its proof only uses the spatial independence, all its parameters are independent of
\rho
. Especially, the decay exponent
\beta
only depends on the dimension
d
.

Lemma 5.4

(Variance decay)
.

There exist
\beta(d)>0
and
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that for every
p,q\in B_{1}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(5.20)

\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}\left(v(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,q)-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
\leqslant C3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}.

Proof.

Since
\rho,p,q
does not change, we write
v_{n}
as a shorthand for
v(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},p,q)
and
v_{n-1,z}
as a shorthand of
v(\rho,z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1},p,q)
. We use a comparison between scale
n
and scale
(n-1)

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda|D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q|.

Here the last term comes from the integration of affine function, and
\chi(\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|
is the correct factor to normalize the integration. We deal with the three terms separately. The third term is naturally bounded

|D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q|\leqslant C(d,\lambda)\tau_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the second term, we use Jensen’s inequality and (
4.23
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tau_{n-1}.

For the first term, we define

X_{z}:=\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v(z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1},p,q))-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right),

and expand the square

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}X_{z}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|^{2}}\sum_{z,w\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\left\langle{X_{z}\cdot X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

Note that
X_{z}
is a
{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
-valued random vector, and here
X_{z}\cdot X_{w}
is the inner product between
X_{z}
and
X_{w}
.

For sufficiently large
n
such that
3^{n}>10\mathbf{r}
, there exist two cubes
z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}
and
w+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n-1}
with distance greater than
2\mathbf{r}
. Recall that
X_{z}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(N_{\mathbf{r}}(z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n}^{+}))
from (1) of Proposition
4.1
. Then we can use the local property and independence for such pair
X_{z},X_{w}
to get

\displaystyle\left\langle{X_{z}\cdot X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=\left\langle{X_{z}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\cdot\left\langle{X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0,

where the last equal sign follows from the average slope property (
4.25
). For other pairs, we just use Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and stationary property, which concludes

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}X_{z}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\frac{3^{2d}-1}{3^{2d}}\left\langle{|X_{0}|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

If we denote left hand side of (
5.20
) by
\sigma_{n}^{2}
, then our calculation above gives

(5.21)

\sigma_{n}\leqslant\theta\sigma_{n-1}+C\tau_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

for some positive constant
\theta:=\frac{3^{2d}-1}{3^{2d}}<1
. This holds for all
n\geqslant n_{0}
such that
3^{n_{0}}>10\mathbf{r}
, and we do iteration to obtain

\displaystyle\forall n>n_{0},\qquad\sigma_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\theta^{n-n_{0}}\sigma_{n_{0}}+C(d,\lambda)\sum_{k=n_{0}}^{n-1}\theta^{n-k}\tau_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})\left(\theta^{n}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\theta^{n-k}\tau_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Here we also use the trivial bound
\sigma_{0}\leqslant\lambda
, and shift the constant. Since there are only finite cases
1\leqslant n\leqslant n_{0}
, we square the equation above and prove (
5.20
).
∎

5.3.
Iterations and track of parameters

In this part, we summarize the previous steps and conclude the proof. Especially, here we need to track the dependence of the density
\rho
in our convergence. Recall (
5.1
), and combine the result (
4.19
) with
\widetilde{D}=D_{m}
defined in (
5.4
),

(5.22)

\begin{split}&\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})\right|\\
&=2\chi(\rho)\left|{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})-{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})\right|\\
&\leqslant C(d,\lambda)\chi(\rho)\left(\sup_{|p|=1}J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).\end{split}

Therefore, we need to study the uniform convergence of
\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)
, where the compresssibility
\chi(\rho)
helps control the convergence near two endpoints. We state the following lemma, which controls
\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)
by the energy gap
\tau_{n}
defined in (
5.5
) uniformly in
\rho
.

Lemma 5.5
.

There exists an exponent
\kappa(d)>0
and a constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that for every
p\in B_{1}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, we have

(5.23)

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\leqslant C\left(3^{-\kappa m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\kappa(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right).

Proof.

We combine the estimates (
5.6
) and (
5.12
), which yield

(5.24)

J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\\
\leqslant C\tau_{m}+C\alpha(\rho)\left(\rho^{-2}\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right)+\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}\right).

This estimate will explode when fixing
m
and sending
\rho
to
0
or
1
, which is even worse than the trivial bound
0\leqslant J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\leqslant 3\lambda
for all
p\in B_{1}
; see (
4.15
) and (2) of Proposition
4.1
. Therefore, we combine these two estimates by an interpolation. Let
s\in(1,\infty)
be an exponent to be determined later, we have

(5.25)

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi^{2}(\rho)J^{\frac{1}{s}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)(3\lambda)^{1-\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\lambda\left(\chi^{2s}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}.

Then we insert the expression (
5.24
). For the case
s\geqslant 2
, we have

\displaystyle\chi^{2s}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\chi^{2s}(\rho)\alpha(\rho)\left(\rho^{-2}\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right)+\chi^{-2}(\rho)3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\chi^{2s-2}(\rho)\alpha(\rho)\left(\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right)+3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}\right).

In the last line, we pay
\chi^{2}(\rho)
to compensate the singularity from
\rho^{-2}
and
\chi^{-2}(\rho)
. Then we study the condition to balance the other term involving
\rho
.

•

Concerning
\alpha(\rho)
, we pose a condition
s\geqslant 3
. Then the factor
(1-\rho)^{2s-2}
from
\chi^{2s-2}(\rho)
dominates
\alpha(\rho)=-\log(1-\rho)
because

\displaystyle(1-\rho)^{2s-2}\alpha(\rho)\leqslant-(1-\rho)\log(1-\rho)\leqslant e^{-1},

and
-x\log x
takes its maximum on
[0,1]
at
x=e^{-1}
.

•

Concerning
3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}
, we pose a condition
s\geqslant 16d+26
. Then the factor
\rho^{2s-2}
from
\chi^{2}(\rho)
can improve the term of concentration estimate

\displaystyle\rho^{2s-2}3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{32d+50}3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{2}\left(\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}\right)^{16d+24}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}.

We use the fact that
x^{16d+24}e^{-\frac{x}{2}}\leqslant C(d)x^{-1}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and the condition
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
from Proposition
5.3
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\rho^{2s-2}3^{(4d+6)m}e^{-\frac{\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}}{2}}\leqslant\rho^{2}\left(\rho^{2}3^{\frac{m}{4}}\right)^{-1}\leqslant 3^{-\frac{m}{4}}\leqslant 3^{-\beta m}.

Thus we set
s=16d+26
, and put these estimates back to (
5.25
), which yields

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{16d+26}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(3^{-\frac{\beta m}{16d+26}}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\frac{\beta m}{16d+26}}\tau_{n}\right).

Here we use
(a+b)^{\frac{1}{n}}\leqslant a^{\frac{1}{n}}+b^{\frac{1}{n}}
for
a,b>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. By setting
\kappa:=\frac{\beta}{16d+26}
, we obtain the desired result (
5.23
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

The remaining part is similar to the proof of
[
11
, Proposition 2.11]
and we give its sketch. We define

(5.26)

\displaystyle F_{m}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},e_{i},D_{m}e_{i}),

and its weighted version with
\kappa>0
from Lemma
5.5
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m}:=\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}(m-n)}F_{n}.

Since (
5.5
) and (
5.23
) all satisfy the estimate independent of
\rho
, it suffices to follow the iteration in
[
11
, Proposition 2.11]
, and there exists a constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m+1}\leqslant C(\widetilde{F}_{m}-\widetilde{F}_{m+1})+C3^{-\frac{\kappa m}{2}}.

This implies a contraction

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m+1}\leqslant\left(\frac{C}{1+C}\right)\widetilde{F}_{m}+\left(\frac{C}{1+C}\right)3^{-\frac{\kappa m}{2}},

which gives the decay of
\widetilde{F}_{m}
. Following (
5.22
) and (
5.26
), there exists
\gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0
and
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that

(5.27)

\displaystyle\forall\rho\in[0,1],\qquad\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})\right|\leqslant F_{m}\leqslant\widetilde{F}_{m}\leqslant C3^{-\gamma_{1}m}.

Recall the monotone convergence in Corollary
4.3
for a fixed
\rho
, thus as
m\to\infty
, the sequence
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
decreases and
\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
increases to the same limit
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)
. Then (
5.27
) gives the desired uniform convergence rate along the triadic cubes, and Lemma
A.1
extends the result to a general cube, which concludes the proof. ∎

6.
Density-free local corrector with uniform convergence

There still remains some difference between our homogenization result in Proposition
5.1
and Theorem
1.2
. The optimizer
F_{L}
in Theorem
1.2
is a corrector-type function in homogenization, and we have already obtained a natural candidate from previous sections (see (
4.1
) and (1) of Proposition
4.1
)

(6.1)

\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}:=v(\rho,\Lambda,\xi)-\ell_{\Lambda^{+},\xi}.

This local corrector is of
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})
, but has the dependence on
\rho
. In this part, we explore various properties about this function at first in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
, then improve it by removing the dependence of density in Section
6.3
, and finally prove the main theorem (Theorem
1.2
) in Section
6.4
and
6.5
.

6.1.
Regularity of local corrector

We summarize at first some properties about our local corrector
\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}
from previous sections.

Proposition 6.1
.

The local corrector
\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}
satisfies the following properties.

(1)

(Elementary properties)
\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}
is a
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})
function with
\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0
, and
\xi\mapsto\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}
is linear.

(2)

(Approximation of conductivity) For every
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

(6.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1],\xi\in B_{1}}\left|\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi\right|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{1}}.

Here the exponent
\gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})
and the constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})
are same as in Proposition
5.1
.

(3)

(Sublinearity) There exists a constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
, such for every
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the following estimate holds for
\gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})
from Proposition
5.1

(6.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1],\xi\in B_{1}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi^{2}_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant CL^{2-\gamma_{1}}.

Proof.

(1) can be deduced from Proposition
4.1
. (2) is the consequence from Proposition
5.1
. (3) comes from Proposition
5.3
, once we put the convergence rate (
5.3
) to (
5.5
).
∎

The main task of this part is to explore the regularity on
\rho
. We propose the following factor to measure the one-sided bias when change the probability

(6.4)

\displaystyle\forall\rho^{\prime},\rho\in(0,1),\qquad\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}:=\max\left\{\frac{\rho^{\prime}}{\rho},\frac{1-\rho^{\prime}}{1-\rho}\right\},

This quantity is used to control the continuity with respect to the small change of density
\rho
. We remark that, the role of
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
is not same here, since we should always view the second parameter as the targeted density and the first parameter as its perturbation. The following observation is obvious

(6.5)

\displaystyle|\rho^{\prime}-\rho|<\varepsilon\min\{\rho,1-\rho\}\Longrightarrow\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}\in[1,1+\varepsilon].

Especially, we only require the targeted density
\rho
not to be degenerated to
0
or
1
to get a good estimate, but the perturbation
\rho^{\prime}
can be degenerate. We also define the two-sided bias factor

(6.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}:=\max\{\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho},\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\},

where the role of
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
is symmetric.

The following lemma is an example to apply the one-sided bias factor
\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}
, and it will be useful throughout the section.

Lemma 6.2
.

For bounded domain
\Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
and every local function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda)
, the following inequality holds for every
\rho^{\prime},\rho\in(0,1)

(6.7)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right|\leqslant\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{|\Lambda|}-1\right)\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\rho}.

Proof.

We make the decomposition using the canonical ensemble. Denote by
X
the random variable
X:=\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\eta_{x}

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]-\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\right)\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]-\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\right|\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M}

\displaystyle=\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]}-1\right|\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M}.

Using the expression of Binomial distribution and the bound
M\leqslant|\Lambda|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]}-1\right|=\left|\frac{(\rho^{\prime})^{M}(1-\rho^{\prime})^{|\Lambda|-M}}{{\rho}^{M}(1-\rho)^{|\Lambda|-M}}-1\right|\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{|\Lambda|}-1.

This concludes the desired result.
∎

We now study the dependence of the density
\rho
in several quantities. Similar argument can be found in
[
36
, Proposition 6.1]
.

Proposition 6.3

(Regularity on density)
.

For every
L\in N_{+}
, every
\rho,\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}\in(0,1)
and
\xi\in B_{1}
, we have the following estimates using the factors
\Theta
and
\widetilde{\Theta}
defined respectively in (
6.4
) and (
6.6
).

(1)

Regularity of conductivity: we have

(6.8)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),.

and

(6.9)

\displaystyle|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})|\leqslant\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}.

(2)

Regularity of mean:

(6.10)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}}\leqslant L^{2}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L})

(3)

Regularity of Dirichlet energy and
L^{2}
: if
\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant 2
, we have

(6.11)

L^{-2}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\
\leqslant 10\left(\max\left\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\right\}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}.

Proof.

(1) We test
\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}
in the variational problem of
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda)
, and apply (
6.7
) to change the parameter of the grand canonical ensemble

(6.12)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})\xi&\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\
&\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}\\
&=\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\frac{1}{2}p\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})p.\end{split}

Here because of the correlation length
\mathbf{r}
, the integration
\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}
is in
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{L+2\mathbf{r}})
and we need to enlarge the power for the factor
\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}
. This proves (
6.8
). By exchanging the role of
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
, we can obtain the estimate on the other direction similarly, which concludes (
6.9
) using
\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}
defined in (
6.6
).

(2) Recall that
\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0
from (1) of Proposition
6.1
, then we apply (
6.7
) to
\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left|\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\left\langle{|\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}|}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\left\langle{\phi^{2}_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leqslant L^{2}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}).

Here we apply Jensen’s inequality from the second line to the third line, and the spectral inequality (
2.5
) from the third line to the forth line.

(3) To compare the Dirichlet energy, we add
\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}
as an intermediate term, which gives

\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\
\leqslant\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}.

For each term, we can repeat the argument in (
6.12
), and conclude that

(6.13)

\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\
\leqslant\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}+\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-2\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}.

The
L^{2}
term can be done similarly,

\left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\
\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}.

Here, we hope to use Poincaré inequality, but the constant part of the function should be truncated. We take the term involving
\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}
for example

\left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\
\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}.

The Poincaré inequality (
2.5
) applies to the first term, and then we can use (
6.13
)

\displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4L^{2}}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4L^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}.

Concerning the term
\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}
, we apply directly (
6.10
). Under the assumption
\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant 2
, then we have
\left(\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\leqslant\left(\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)
and the leading order should be
\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)
. This concludes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Convergence rate under canonical ensemble

In this part, we give the convergence rate of the conductivity under the canonical ensemble. Inspired by the subadditive quantities in (
4.1
), we can also define their counterparts under the canonical ensemble that

(6.14)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}p\cdot\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N)p&:=\inf_{v\in\ell_{p,\Lambda^{+}}+\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})}\left\{\frac{1}{2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,N}\right\},\\
\frac{1}{2}q\cdot\widehat{D}_{*}^{-1}(\Lambda,N)q\\
&:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{F}_{0}}\left\{\frac{1}{2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\Lambda}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q,\Lambda})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,N}\right\}.\end{split}

Similarly to (
5.1
), we define the conductivity using the Einstein relation (
1.12
)

(6.15)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda,N):=2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N),\qquad\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\Lambda,N):=2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda,N).

Here
\hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda,N)
also coincides with the definition (
1.25
). Our main result in this subsection is the following proposition.

Proposition 6.4
.

Under Hypothesis
1.1
, there exists an exponent
\gamma_{2}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0
and a positive constant
C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty
such that for every
L,M\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(6.16)

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda_{L},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)\right|+\left|\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)\right|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{2}},

where
\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)
is the same as that defined in (
5.2
).

We establish at first a result of the local equivalence of ensembles. Similar result can be found in
[
51
, Appendix 2]
and other references. In our setting, for any
\Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we define the following set of integers such that the empirical density is not degenerate

(6.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda):=\{M\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:\varepsilon\leqslant M/|\Lambda|\leqslant 1-\varepsilon\}.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
L,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
M\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
\hat{\rho}
the empirical density
\hat{\rho}:=\frac{M}{|\Lambda_{L}|}
.

(1)

If
10\ell^{2}\leqslant L
and
0\leqslant M\leqslant L^{d}
, then for any local function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell})
such that
{\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}\geqslant 0}
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}\leqslant\left(1+4\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}\right)\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}.

(2)

Given
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, if
10\ell^{2d}\leqslant\varepsilon L^{d}
and
M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda_{L})
, then for any local function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell})
, we have

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
6.2
. For
f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell})
, we decompose the expectation as

(6.20)

\displaystyle\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}=\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}-1\right)\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}.

It remains to analyze the Radon–Nikodym derivative under different setting.

(1) Under this setting, because
\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}
is positive, it suffice to have an upper bound for the following probability

(6.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]=\frac{{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|\choose M-N}}{{|\Lambda_{L}|\choose M}}=\frac{\left(\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\right)\left(\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\right)}{\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}}.

Notice that

(6.22)

\begin{split}&\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}\geqslant|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|},\qquad\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\leqslant M^{N},\\
&\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\leqslant(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N},\end{split}

thus we have an upper bound for (
6.21
)

(6.23)

\begin{split}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}&\leqslant\left(\frac{M^{N}(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}}\right)\Big/\left(\frac{M^{N}(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}}{|\Lambda_{L}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}.\end{split}

It suffices to give an estimate for the term
\left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}
. Using the condition
{\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\in(0,\frac{1}{10})}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}\leqslant\left(1+2\left(\frac{\ell}{L}\right)^{d}\right)^{\ell^{d}}\leqslant e^{\log\left(1+2\left(\frac{\ell}{L}\right)^{d}\right)\ell^{d}}\leqslant e^{2\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}}\leqslant 1+4\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}.

This estimate together with (
6.21
), (
6.23
) and (
6.18
) gives us the desired result for positive function
f
.

(2) Under this setting,
\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}
can be negative, so we need two-sided estimate for the Radon-Nikodym derivative. The upper bound is already proved in (
6.23
), and we only need the lower bound, which is as follows

(6.24)

\begin{split}&\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|},\qquad\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\geqslant(M-N)^{N},\\
&\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\geqslant((|\Lambda_{L}|-M)-(|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N))^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}.\end{split}

This gives us a lower bound of the proportion

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}\geqslant\left(1-\frac{N}{M}\right)^{N}\left(1-\frac{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}{|\Lambda_{L}|-M}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}.

Because
M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda_{L})
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leqslant\max\left\{\frac{N}{M},\frac{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}{|\Lambda_{L}|-M}\right\}\leqslant\frac{\ell^{d}}{\varepsilon L^{d}},

which results in

(6.25)

\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}\geqslant\left(1-\frac{\ell^{d}}{\varepsilon L^{d}}\right)^{\ell^{d}}\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}.

Here we also make use of condition
10\ell^{2d}\leqslant\varepsilon L^{d}
. This estimate and (
6.20
) together conclude (
6.19
).
∎

With this local equivalence of ensembles result, we can obtain the convergence rate of diffusion matrix under the canonical ensemble.

Proof of Proposition
6.4
.

By similar analysis as Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.6
in previous sections, we obtain that

(6.26)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda,N)\leqslant\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N)\leqslant\lambda\mathsf{Id}.

The strategy is the following
sandwich argument
: we prove that in scale
1\ll\ell\ll L
, we have

(6.27)

{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})-C\ell L^{-1}\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\\
\leqslant\widehat{D}(\Lambda_{L},M)\leqslant{\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})+C\ell L^{-1}\mathsf{Id}.

Then the distance from
{\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})
(resp.
{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})
) to
{\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)
bounds that from
\widehat{D}(\Lambda_{L},M)
(resp.
\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)
) to
{\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)
.

In the following, for the convenience to implement the renormalization step, we justify the two sides in (
6.27
) with
L=3^{m},\ell=3^{n},{m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
, but one can easily adapt it to the general case. We always suppose that the parameters
L=3^{m},\ell=3^{n}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
6.5
. We also denote by
\hat{\rho}:=\frac{M}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}
to lighten the notation, which can be interpreted as the empirical density of particles under the canonical ensemble.

Step 1: comparison between
\widehat{D}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},M)
and
{\overbracket{D}}(\hat{\rho},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n})
.
We propose a sub-minimizer
\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\xi}
for the optimization problem of
\widehat{D}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},M)
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\xi}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\
\dist(z,\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{n},\xi}.

Here we require the
\dist(z,\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})>3^{n}
in order to cutoff the influence of particles from the domain outside
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}
. Since it is a sub-minimizer, we have

(6.28)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\widehat{D}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},M)\xi\\
